 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">As used in this Annual Report on Form 10-K, &#147;we&#148;, &#147;our&#148;, &#147;us&#148;,
the &#147;Company&#148; and &#147;TRC&#148; all refer to Technology Research Corporation and its subsidiary unless the context otherwise requires. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>DISCLOSURE REGARDING FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS </I></B></FONT></P>

Act of 1995 and the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, and any forward looking statements made herein are based on our current expectations, involve a number of risks and uncertainties and should not be considered as guarantees of future
performance.&nbsp;Such statements may be identified by terminology such as &#147;may,&#148; &#147;will,&#148; &#147;should,&#148; &#147;expects,&#148; &#147;scheduled,&#148; &#147;plans,&#148; &#147;intends,&#148; &#147;anticipates,&#148;
&#147;believes,&#148; &#147;estimates,&#148; &#147;potential,&#148; or &#147;continue,&#148; or the negative of such terms, or other comparable terminology.&nbsp;These statements are only predictions, and actual events as well as results may differ
materially.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In evaluating these statements, you should specifically consider the information described in the &#147;<I>Risk Factors&#148;
</I>and other documents we file from time to time with the Securities and Exchange Commission such as our quarterly reports on Form 10-Q and our current reports on Form 8-K. Other key factors include, but are not limited to, the acceptance of any

<P STYLE="margin-top:24px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><U><A NAME="toc23282_1"></A>PART I </U></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="9%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><A NAME="toc23282_2"></A>ITEM&nbsp;1.</B></FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>BUSINESS </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>OVERVIEW
</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Technology Research Corporation is a recognized leader in providing cost effective engineered solutions for
applications involving power management and control, intelligent battery systems technology and electrical safety products based on our proven ground fault sensing and <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> technology. These products are designed, manufactured and
distributed to the consumer, commercial and industrial markets worldwide. The Company also supplies power monitors and control equipment to the United States Military and its prime contractors. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">TRC was incorporated in Florida in 1981.&nbsp;Our principal offices are located at 5250-140th Avenue North, Clearwater, Florida 33760,
our telephone number is (727)&nbsp;535-0572 and our website can be accessed at <U>www.trci.net</U>.&nbsp;Information contained or referenced on our website is not incorporated by reference into, and does not form a part of, this Annual Report on
Form 10-K. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In fiscal 2010, we undertook a strategic planning process that included a comprehensive review of our existing
products, customers and the markets in which we compete over a strategic planning horizon of three years. During this process, it became evident that many of the current markets for electrical safety products in which we compete are mature, with
limited opportunities for growth in the future. In order for us to achieve sustainable long-term growth, and increase shareholder value, management believes we must move into markets that allow for higher margins and less commoditization. We intend
to continue to focus resources on our key market sectors: military, transportation/specialty vehicle, and industrial/commercial markets. These markets have many of the same needs and will allow us to converge much of our development talent to
product platforms that will serve each of these markets. We will continue to take advantage of our positions in the mature military mobile generator set and ground fault sensing markets which should generate substantial cash flow over the next three
years. This cash flow will help us transition the business to include higher-growth markets that will sustain revenue growth in future years as the markets for our electrical safety products mature. We have undertaken a number of initiatives to
lower costs, improve asset turnover and reduce our risk.&nbsp;These initiatives include, but are not limited to, (i)&nbsp;simplifying our product line, (ii)&nbsp;designing standard product platforms, (iii)&nbsp;expanding the utilization of our
operations in Honduras, and (iv)&nbsp;developing new designs and operations software to improve quality, accelerate product development and reduce the cost of our redesigned products. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Over our three-year strategic horizon, our plan is to be a profitable niche player in the power management and power storage markets with
projected double-digit growth rates. Management believes this will require us to develop skills to migrate from an electromechanical ground fault company to a microprocessor-based developer of power products that serve our military,
transportation/specialty vehicle, and industrial markets. We intend to develop or acquire skills that focus on four key technologies to drive growth. These include: </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>&#149;</B></FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Products that convert Alternating Current (AC) to Direct Current (DC) and convert DC to AC </B></FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">1 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">With the proliferation of electronics products, the use of inverters is continuing to grow.
The latest technology to support these electronics devices is generally referred to as pure sine wave, a process which converts DC power to AC power. While we currently build pure sine wave inverters for military generator sets, these products use
older electromechanical technologies that are not competitive in a commercial or renewable energy environment. Although our use of this technology historically has been focused on military generator sets, we intend to expand our capabilities to
utilize this technology for our inverter products. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>&#149;</B></FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Converting AC power into DC power while optimizing the life of the batteries being charged. </B></FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The demand for battery charging products continues to rise for consumers, industrial and military applications. We intend to utilize our
in-house skills, together with acquired engineering talent to enhance our development of this technology. Our experience and capabilities in serving the military sector provides us with an excellent entry into this market. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>&#149;</B></FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Developing intelligence and communications products through the use of microprocessor based technology. </B></FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">By upgrading and developing our firmware and through the expansion of engineering resources, we plan to expand our Network Digital
Switching (NDS) multiplexing product line in fiscal year 2011. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>

<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>&#149;</B></FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Power management and distribution technologies. </B></FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">With TRC&#146;s experience in developing electrical safety products that provide ground fault sensing capabilities, we intend to
transition our product mix to include products utilizing intelligent power distribution for the use of micro grid applications for the military and commercial market segments. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">On March&nbsp;31, 2010, we completed an acquisition of all of the common stock of Patco Electronics, Inc., a Titusville, Florida based
company (&#147;Patco&#148;). Patco designs and manufactures battery management tools for secondary or re-chargeable batteries in several battery chemistries, including lead acid and lithium ion. Patco&#146;s product solutions support customers in
military, commercial and industrial sectors. The acquisition of Patco greatly enhances our ability to convert AC power into DC current that optimizes the life of the batteries being charged, thereby enabling us to take advantage of one of the four
technologies identified in our strategic plan to drive business growth. For its calendar year ended December&nbsp;31, 2009, Patco revenue was approximately $6 million and its net income was approximately $1.7 million. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We plan to&nbsp;pursue&nbsp;our operating strategy; however,&nbsp;actual results could differ materially from those projected or assumed
in any of&nbsp;our forward-looking statements within this report.&nbsp;Our future financial condition and results of operations, as well as our operational and financial expectations, are subject to inherent risks and uncertainties.&nbsp;Some, but
not all, of the factors impacting these risks and uncertainties are set forth below in the section&nbsp;entitled &#147;Risk Factors.&#148; </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We file reports with the Securities and Exchange Commission (&#147;<B>SEC</B>&#148;), including annual reports on Form 10-K, quarterly
reports on Form 10-Q, and current reports on Form 8-K.&nbsp;Copies of our reports filed with the SEC may be obtained by the public at the SEC&#146;s Public Reference Room at 100 F Street, NE, Washington, D.C.&nbsp;20549, or by calling the SEC at
1-800-SEC-0330.&nbsp;The information provided on our website is not part of this report, and is therefore not incorporated by reference unless such information is otherwise specifically referenced elsewhere in this report. </FONT></P>

<TD VALIGN="bottom" ALIGN="right"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">45,620</FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our backlog of
unshipped orders as of March&nbsp;31, 2010 was approximately $9.2 million, as compared to approximately $15.2 million as of March&nbsp;31, 2009.&nbsp;This backlog consists of approximately 19% of commercial product orders and approximately 81% of
military product orders,&nbsp;including approximately $2.3 million from Patco, all of which&nbsp;are expected to ship within the year ending March&nbsp;31, 2011.&nbsp;The decrease in backlog is principally due to our prime military customer
releasing full calendar year orders prior to the closing of our fiscal 2009 year, resulting in the prior year backlog being uncharacteristically high. Our unshipped backlog orders include orders for products where written customer requests have been
accepted and the delivery of products is anticipated within the next 12 months.&nbsp;Our policy is to make adjustments to our backlog to reflect, among other things, customer delivery date changes as well as order cancellations.&nbsp;We schedule
production based upon purchase orders in backlog and our customer&#146;s delivery requirements.&nbsp;Generally, our orders may be changed, rescheduled or cancelled with limited penalties prior to shipment, although our military backlog is generally
non-cancellable.&nbsp;For these reasons, our backlog at any particular date is not necessarily indicative of future sales for any succeeding period. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Commercial Products and Markets </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Core Commercial Products.</I>&nbsp;Our core commercial business was developed out of the demand for the following Underwriters
Laboratories (&#147;<B>UL</B>&#148;) classifications of ground fault protective devices:&nbsp;Ground Fault Circuit Interrupters (&#147;<B>GFCI</B>&#148;);&nbsp;Equipment Leakage Current Interrupters (&#147;<B>ELCI</B>&#148;); Portable Residual
Current Devices (&#147;<B>PRCD</B>&#148;); and Leakage Current Detection Interrupters (&#147;<B>LCDI</B>&#148;).</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Ground fault
protective devices help protect against the hazards of fire and electrical shock that result when water comes in contact with electrically &#147;live&#148; conductors or when faulty electrical grounding is found in old or damaged extension cords,
appliance cords, house wiring and electrical equipment.&nbsp;The demand for&nbsp;our commercial products has resulted from the National Electrical Code (&#147;<B>NEC</B>&#148;) requirements, UL product standards and voluntary efforts by industry to
improve the electrical safety of commercial products. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Electrical safety is compromised when a ground fault occurs, which is a
condition where electric current finds an abnormal path to ground, such as when a power tool comes in contact with water while plugged into a live outlet or when it is damaged in such a way as to cause internal wiring to come in contact with exposed
metal parts allowing electricity to pass through the user of that power tool.&nbsp;Upon such occurrence, the entire device can become as electrically alive as the power line to which it is attached. If a person is touching such a live device while
grounded (by being in contact with the ground or, for example, a metal pipe, gas pipe, drain or any attached metal device), that person can be seriously or fatally injured by electric shock.&nbsp;Fuses or circuit breakers do not provide adequate
protection against such shock, because the amount of current necessary to injure or kill a human or animal is far below the level of current required for a fuse to blow or a circuit breaker to trip. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our GFCI, ELCI and PRCD&nbsp;products are all ground fault devices providing protection from dangerous electrical shock by sensing
leakage of electricity and cutting off power.&nbsp;Ground fault devices&nbsp;are currently available in three types: circuit breaker, receptacle and portable.&nbsp;We specialize in the portable types of these products.&nbsp;Ground fault
devices&nbsp;constantly monitor electric current, and as long as the amount of current returning from the device is equal to the amount that is directed to the device, the GFCI performs no activities. Conversely, if there is less current coming back
than there is flowing into the device, some portion must be taking a path through a foreign body, thereby creating a hazard.&nbsp;Upon recognizing that condition, the&nbsp;ground fault device&nbsp;terminates the flow of electricity instantaneously.
Ground fault devices range in rating from 120V/15A to 600V/80A (the largest are part of&nbsp;our unique HD-PRO family of products).</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our LCDI devices are intended to reduce the risk of electrical fires by disconnecting power when sensing current leakage between
conductors of power cords.&nbsp;Our <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> product lines are approved in the UL classification of LCDIs. Several years ago, both government and industry research into the major causes of fire led to a search for new, cost-effective
methods to prevent electrical fires. In response to this need,&nbsp;we developed and patented <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I>, a product designed to prevent fires caused by damaged or aging appliance power supply cords and extension cords, which have been
identified as a leading cause of electrical fires.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">3 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> technology currently addresses four distinct market
applications: (i)&nbsp;the <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> Power Surge Strip - a consumer product; (ii)&nbsp;the <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> Safety Circuit - an OEM product; (iii)&nbsp;the <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> Power Cord - an OEM product; and (iv)&nbsp;the
<I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> Safety Extension Cord - a consumer product. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our line of <I>Surge Guard</I>&#153; and <I>Surge Guard
Plus</I>&#153; products are designed to meet the rigorous requirements of the recreational vehicle (&#147;<B>RV</B>&#148;) market. These products provide&nbsp;power protection and have both OEM and after-market applications.&nbsp;In
addition,&nbsp;we&nbsp;have introduced the Surge Guard Automatic Transfer Switch, which incorporates a transfer switch into the functionality of our Surge Guard products,&nbsp;thus eliminating the need for two separate products.</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our 30 Amp and 50 Amp Surge Guard RV Voltage Regulators are part of our power protection product line and used in recreational vehicles
to provide a reliable source of power when experiencing low RV park input voltage. This product automatically and continually monitors line voltage conditions and will boost voltage to an acceptable level to help prevent low voltage damage to the
RV&#146;s appliances and electronic devices. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In fiscal 2010, we introduced a network digital switching product family for the
RV and specialty transportation vehicle markets that eventually will provide power management of lighting, transfer switches, room slide-outs, pumps, awnings, security features , diagnostics and other user information. These products are
microprocessor control based and rely upon embedded software (firmware) to provide programmable devices that can communicate reducing wiring and harnessing requirements throughout the vehicle. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Research, Development and Engineering </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We place substantial emphasis on new product development and believe that continued investment in product development is required to
maintain and improve our competitive position.&nbsp;Our product development activities emphasize new proprietary products, enhancement of existing products and process technologies. Our research and product development activities occur in
Clearwater, Florida and in Titusville, Florida.&nbsp;Our Engineering Department is engaged in designing and developing new commercial and military products and improving existing products to meet the needs of&nbsp;our customers. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In connection with&nbsp;our efforts&nbsp;to develop&nbsp;new products,&nbsp;we have directed our focus on &#147;Engineer to
Engineer&#148; type solutions.&nbsp;What this means is that we are working on long term type contracts where we supply our technology to other OEMs for the equipment and products they manufacture.&nbsp;The OEM equipment can be for commercial,
industrial or military applications, but is usually application specific in nature.&nbsp;We have developed a power management system architecture that can be used for power management of lighting, transfer switches, room slide-outs, pumps, awnings
security features, diagnostics and other user information. In fiscal 2011, we will continue to roll-out our Network Digital Switching product line which is microprocessor based, one of the key strategic initiatives that we identified in our
strategic planning process. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In addition to our focus on new product development, we continue to look at ways to reduce cost
and improve the value proposition to our customers with our existing products. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We spent $2.6 million, $2.4 million and $1.9
million in fiscal 2010, 2009 and 2008, respectively, on research, development and engineering activities, and&nbsp;we anticipate spending levels at approximately&nbsp;7% of revenue in fiscal 2011. All engineering activities are expensed as incurred.
</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Marketing, Sales and Distribution</B> </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our products are sold throughout the world, primarily through an in-house sales force, through product licensing agreements&nbsp;and sales
and marketing agreements.&nbsp;We will continue to market existing and new products through these channels.&nbsp;In addition,&nbsp;we&nbsp;are looking for other viable channels through which to market&nbsp;our products.&nbsp;We rely heavily upon the
marketing skills and experience, as well as the business experience, of&nbsp;our management&nbsp;personnel in marketing&nbsp;our products. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We complement&nbsp;our sales and marketing activities through the use of additional distributors and sales representative
organizations.&nbsp;Our sales efforts in the industrial/construction market is supported by utilizing approximately 30 independent sales representatives who sell to over 1,500 electrical, industrial and safety distributors.&nbsp;The majority of our
sales, however, are made through our in-house sales force.&nbsp;We exhibit&nbsp;our products at numerous trade shows, which have resulted in new commercial markets including the recreational vehicle industry and the appliance industry. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We also market through OEMs, both domestically and internationally, that sell&nbsp;our products as a component of an end user product or
under their own brand names (&#147;private label&#148;).&nbsp;We continue to implement a &#147;value-add&#148; upgrade strategy, which provides a finished product to those who private label&nbsp;our products and who are currently only receiving
subassembly modules.&nbsp;Our plastic and receptacle molding capabilities are a key factor in providing &#147;value add&#148; upgrades to&nbsp;our customers. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">4 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>

<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Commercial/Industrial Markets.</I></B>&nbsp;We market and sell our GFCI, ELCI
(including the heavy duty HD-PRO products), LCDI and our cable protection and management products to the commercial/industrial market.&nbsp;This market is served by over 1,500 electrical and safety distributors and a number of catalogers. These
electrical distributors include branches of major chains as well as smaller independent distributors. Catalogers range from safety to industrial and electrical suppliers. We also&nbsp;make private label products for a number of industry leaders to
help penetrate this market. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Consumer/Retail Markets.</I></B>&nbsp;We currently market and sell various portable GFCI,
LCDI and other specialty products to the consumer market.&nbsp;Our major retail customers include Wal-Mart, Home Depot, Meijers, and&nbsp;Ace Hardware, as well as with many independent retailers. Our products are also being offered through
magazines, catalogs and E-commerce retailers such as Amazon.com.&nbsp;We continue to have success selling our aftermarket RV Surge Guard products through retailers such as Camping World and distributors. We are now one of Camping World&#146;s top 30
suppliers. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In fiscal 2010, we&nbsp;continued our growth&nbsp;and marketing in niche markets such as in the college and
university segment of the consumer market with our <I>Fire Shield&reg;</I> LCDI products. A number of colleges and universities are either mandating, or strongly suggesting the use of LCDI protected extension cords and surge strips on campus. In
turn, this helps drive our revenue through retailers. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Original Equipment Manufacturers (OEMs).</I></B>&nbsp;We
primarily sell GFCI, ELCI, LCDI and RV Surge Guard and Surge Guard Plus products to this market. The OEM market can also be significantly impacted by new and revised product standards as outlined below: </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Impact of New and Revised Product Standards.</I></B>&nbsp;The NEC requires ground fault protection for many applications, which are
enforced by OSHA and local government building codes and adhered to by most manufacturers.&nbsp;We presently focus&nbsp;our marketing efforts in certain spot markets, which have developed in response to NEC imposed requirements.&nbsp;The NEC
requirements are often incorporated into UL product standards. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">An important change in OSHA Regulations in 2007 broadened the
requirements for using portable GFCIs in most industries. This new regulation extends GFCI use requirements for the first time to OSHA&#146;s General Industry category of businesses which cover most of the rest of U.S. industry with the exception of
agriculture, mining and maritime. Now, in order to comply with OSHA Regulations, a great deal of maintenance, remodeling or repair activities involving buildings, structures or equipment must be done with workers using GFCI protected equipment. Our
sales personnel and representative organizations are finding a significant amount of new opportunities for sales of the Company&#146;s Shock Shield&reg; and TRC Power products to industrial supply distributors. Other distribution channels catering
to buyers of equipment and supplies throughout U.S. industry are also being targeted. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>License
Agreements.</I></B>&nbsp;From time to time, we&nbsp;enter into license and sales and marketing agreements concerning&nbsp;our portable GFCI, ELCI and LCDI products.&nbsp;These agreements&nbsp;are&nbsp;intended&nbsp;to assist&nbsp;our&nbsp;market
penetration into those areas where it would be difficult for&nbsp;us to compete on a direct basis and provide the best return on investment to us. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Military Products and Markets </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We design and manufacture products for sale to the military engine generator set controls market.&nbsp;Our expertise in this area is
well-known, and&nbsp;our performance in product quality and delivery to the United States military and its prime contractors have resulted in&nbsp;our being recognized as a leader in this industry.&nbsp;The Defense Logistics Agency established a
program rating system for its suppliers in 1995 for product quality, packaging and on-time deliveries, and since its inception and for the fourteenth straight year,&nbsp;we have been honored as a Best Value Medalist for the highest rating Gold
Category, which signifies&nbsp;our commitment to military contract performance. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We&nbsp;are currently a supplier of control
equipment used in engine generator systems purchased by the United States military and its prime contractors. The term &#147;control equipment&#148; refers to the electrical devices used to control the electrical power output of the generating
systems. In general, the controls monitor and regulate the operation of engine generator mobile electric generating system sets.&nbsp;Electric generating systems are basic to all branches of the military, and demand&nbsp;is generally less volatile
than&nbsp;products utilized in armaments and missiles.&nbsp;Sales are made either directly to the government for support parts or to prime contractors for new production electric generator sets which incorporate&nbsp;our products.&nbsp;We&nbsp;are a
qualified supplier for&nbsp;more than 50 control equipment products as required by the U.S. Department of Defense and&nbsp;serve as&nbsp;a supplier of the following types of control equipment, among others: protective relays and relay assemblies,
instrumentation transducer controls, fault locating panel indicators, current and power transformer assemblies for current sensing control and instrumentation, motor operated circuit breaker assemblies and electrical load board and voltage change
board assemblies.&nbsp;These products are also furnished for spare parts support for existent systems in the military inventory. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In 1989,&nbsp;we completed the redesign of the control equipment related to&nbsp;the 5/10/15/30/60KW Tactical Quiet Generator
(&#147;<B>TQG</B>&#148;) Systems programs.&nbsp;We&nbsp;are actively supplying these parts to DRS Technologies, Inc. (&#147;<B>DRS</B>&#148;) and L-3 Communications Inc., who are the prime contractors.&nbsp;In addition,&nbsp;we&nbsp;are also
supplying to&nbsp;DRS control equipment related to the 3KW and the 100-200KW TQG systems program which first began in November 1998.&nbsp;The 3KW program, which accounted for approximately $3.7 million in fiscal 2010 revenue, is currently under
contract with DRS Technologies until 2012 and is expected to be part of a </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">5 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">
competitive procurement by the Army for a follow-on contract potentially running through 2017. We are actively involved with Letterkenny Army Depot in support of overhauling and refurbishment of
the 3KW power inverters being returned from Iraq and Afghanistan. We also supply products for maintenance and spare parts support directly to the U.S. military. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We furnish various types of A.C. power voltage and frequency monitors to the military for its U.S. Navy vessels.&nbsp;These monitors
provide system protection for the 400 Hz. electrical distribution systems that are used on all classes of U.S. Navy surface vessels, such as minesweepers, destroyers, guided missile cruisers and aircraft carriers in addition to other types of naval
vessels.&nbsp;The monitors meet the environmental and stringent U.S. Navy high shock, vibration and endurance testing requirements, and they are furnished for new vessel production, retrofit upgrades and existing vessel&nbsp;replacement parts
support. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our newly acquired subsidiary, Patco, designs and manufactures battery management tools for secondary or
re-chargeable batteries in several battery chemistries, including lead acid and lithium ion. Patco&#146;s product solutions support customers in military, commercial and industrial sectors. To date, Patco&#146;s revenue has been concentrated in
military training commands both domestically as well as internationally. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our contracts with the U.S. Government are on a
fixed-price bid basis.&nbsp;As with all fixed-price contracts, whether government or commercial,&nbsp;we may not be able to negotiate higher prices to cover losses should unexpected manufacturing costs be incurred. All government contracts contain a
provision that allows for cancellation by the government &#147;for convenience.&#148;&nbsp;However, the government must pay for costs incurred and a percentage of profits expected if a contract is canceled.</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Testing and Qualification </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">A number of&nbsp;our commercial products must be tested and approved by UL or an approved testing laboratory.&nbsp;UL publishes certain
&#147;Standards of Safety&#148; which various types of products must meet and&nbsp;requires that&nbsp;specific tests be undertaken to ascertain whether the products meet the prescribed standards. If a product passes these tests, it receives UL
approval. Once&nbsp;our products have been initially tested and qualified by UL, they are subject to regular field checks and quarterly reviews and evaluations. UL may withdraw its approval for such products if they fail to pass these tests and if
prompt corrective action is not taken.&nbsp;Our portable electrical safety products have received UL approval. In addition, certain of&nbsp;our portable GFCI and ELCI products have successfully undergone similar testing procedures conducted by
comparable governmental testing facilities in Europe, Canada and Japan. We are a member of several committees (UL, IEC, NEMA, CANENA) relating to the establishment of standards requirements for electrical safety devices for both global and North
American markets. We also serve on committees for the Recreational Vehicle Industry Association, the Recreational Vehicle Aftermarket Association and the Government Liaison Committee for the Electrical Generating Systems association. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our military products are subject to testing and qualification standards imposed by the U. S. Government.&nbsp;We have established a
quality control system, which has been qualified by the United States Department of Defense to operate under the requirements of a particular specification (MIL-I-45208).&nbsp;To the extent&nbsp;we design a product that&nbsp;we believe meets those
specifications,&nbsp;we submit the product to the responsible government-testing laboratory.&nbsp;Upon issue of the qualification approval and source listing, the product is rarely subject to re-qualification; however, the U. S. Government may
disqualify a product if it is subject to frequent or excessive operational failures.&nbsp;In addition,&nbsp;our governmental contracts provide that the current specifications and requirements could be changed at any time, which could require&nbsp;us
to redesign&nbsp;our existing products or&nbsp;to develop new products which would have to be submitted for testing and qualification prior to their approval for purchase by the military or its prime contractors.&nbsp;Certain contracts also require
witness testing and acceptance by government inspectors prior to shipment of the product. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our wholly-owned foreign
subsidiary, TRC/Honduras S.A. de C.V., is an ISO&nbsp;9000:2000 certified manufacturing facility and an approved supplier to several major corporations, and holds UL, Canadian Standards Association (&#147;<B>CSA</B>&#148;) and the German standards
association, Verband Deutsher Elektrotechniker (&#147;<B>VDE</B>&#148;), approvals. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our manufacturing facility in Clearwater,
Florida is an ISO 9000:2008 certified manufacturing facility. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Environmental Regulations </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our operations involve the use of hazardous and toxic materials and&nbsp;are subject to federal, state and local laws governing the use,
storage and handling of such materials.&nbsp;We&nbsp;fall under the C<I>onditionally Exempt Small Quantity Generators Rule</I> as defined by the Environmental Protection Agency (&#147;<B>EPA</B>&#148;) due to the small amounts of hazardous waste we
generate each year, and the cost&nbsp;of disposing such materials is not material to&nbsp;our financial condition, results of operations or cash flows.&nbsp;We believe we are in general compliance with these regulations and that we have obtained all
necessary permits to operate our business. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">6 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Design and Manufacturing </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We currently design almost all of the products that&nbsp;we produce and generally will not undertake special design work for customers
unless&nbsp;we receive a contract or purchase order to produce the resulting products.&nbsp;A significant number of&nbsp;our commercial and military electronic products are specialized in that they combine both electronic and magnetic features in
design and production. As we begin to execute on our strategic plan and introduce intelligent products to the marketplace, more of our added-value is in the firmware, which is proprietary and difficult to copy. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The business of an electronics manufacturer primarily involves assembly of component parts.&nbsp;We mold most of&nbsp;our own plastic
parts for&nbsp;our commercial product lines at&nbsp;our manufacturing facility in Honduras.&nbsp;The remainder of the products that&nbsp;we manufacture is assembled from component parts that are produced or distributed by other companies.
</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our wholly-owned subsidiary, TRC/Honduras, S.A. de C.V., manufactures&nbsp;most of our high-volume products sold in the
U.S.&nbsp;TRC/Honduras, S.A. de C.V. leases&nbsp;47,000 square feet&nbsp;of property&nbsp;which is located in ZIP San Jose, a free trade zone and industrial park, located in San Pedro Sula, Honduras.&nbsp;We also manufacture some component parts for
our military business in Honduras as well. The benefits of being located in a free trade zone include no Honduran duties on imported raw materials or equipment, no sales or export tax on exported finished products, no U.S. income taxes incurred for
any profits generated by the subsidiary, and various other benefits. We utilize our subsidiary in Honduras and Asian contract manufacturers to provide quality products to customers at our most competitive prices </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our newly acquired wholly-owned subsidiary, Patco, is engaged in the design and manufacture of intelligent battery management tools in
its Titusville, Florida facility. The battery cells packaged by Patco are purchased from third part manufacturers and are delivered to Patco as sealed cells. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We continue to manufacture&nbsp;our military products and distribute certain of&nbsp;our commercial products, which are manufactured
off-shore, through&nbsp;our 43,000 square foot facility in Clearwater, Florida. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Patents and Trademarks </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our policy is to seek patents on inventions relating to new or enhanced products and processes developed as part of our ongoing research,
engineering, manufacturing and support activities. We seek to protect our products and technologies primarily through patents, trade secrecy measures, copyrights, trademark registrations, licensing restrictions, confidentiality agreements and other
contractual arrangements that are designed to protect our proprietary information.&nbsp;No assurances can be given that others will not independently develop competitive technology that is not covered by our intellectual property rights or that any
measures that we take to protect our proprietary products will be effective. We currently own and hold a number of U.S. patents related to certain products, of which five have been issued in the past two years. We have also filed other patent
applications and are awaiting action on those applications.&nbsp;Our U.S. patents are valid for 14 to 20 years depending on the type of patent and the effective date of application. There can be no assurance that pending patent applications or other
application that we may file will result in issued patents, or that any issued patents will survive challenges to their validity. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The lives of certain patents, related to&nbsp;our ground fault technology, have expired, or will expire within the next few years which
could&nbsp;affect&nbsp;our business in certain markets.&nbsp;We believe the success of&nbsp;our business depends on&nbsp;our technical and engineering expertise, and marketing and service abilities of&nbsp;our employees.&nbsp;We vigorously
protect&nbsp;our patents, but there can be no assurance that others will not independently develop similar products, duplicate&nbsp;our products or design around the patents issued to&nbsp;us.</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We have registered&nbsp;our TRC&reg; trademark with the U.S. Patent and Trademark Office.&nbsp;Our Shock Shield&reg;, Electra Shield&reg;
and Fire Shield&reg; brand names are also U.S. registered trademarks. Our industry is commonly exposed to claims and litigation involving patent and other intellectual property rights, including claims for indemnification arising out of contractual
arrangements with customers.&nbsp;Litigation is often necessary to enforce patent rights or other intellectual property rights. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>

<TD VALIGN="bottom" STYLE="border-top:3px double #000000">&nbsp;</TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our military product sales are primarily to military
procurement logistic agencies for field service support on previously shipped military equipment and to OEM prime contractors of electric generators.&nbsp;In fiscal 2010, military&nbsp;revenue was approximately 57% of total revenue, compared to
approximately 44% in fiscal 2009 and 38% in fiscal 2008.&nbsp;In fiscal 2010, year to&nbsp;year direct sales to the U.S. military&nbsp;increased&nbsp;approximately 23%, sales to&nbsp;DRS increased approximately 11%, and overall,
military&nbsp;revenue increased approximately 34% from fiscal 2009 to fiscal 2010.&nbsp;When comparing fiscal 2009 with fiscal 2008, direct sales to the U.S. military decreased approximately 45%, sales to DRS increased approximately 47%, and
overall, military revenue increased approximately 3%.&nbsp;Direct U.S. military&nbsp;revenue accounted for approximately 13% of our total revenue while&nbsp;DRS accounted for approximately 33% of&nbsp;our total&nbsp;revenue for fiscal 2010, as
compared to approximately 10% and 30%, respectively, for fiscal 2009, and approximately 17% and 19%, respectively, for fiscal 2008. Additionally, in fiscal 2010, DRS also contracted with us through subcontractors, which combined with DRS revenue,
would have accounted for 37% of our revenue in fiscal 2010. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our exports were&nbsp;down approximately 58% in fiscal 2010, as
compared to the prior year, primarily due to&nbsp;our decision to exit the room air conditioner (&#147;RAC&#148;) business in June 2009 and to rely upon a licensing strategy for this market. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We offer&nbsp;our customers no specific product liability protection except with regard to those customers that are specifically named as
&#147;Broad Form Vendors&#148; under&nbsp;our product liability coverage.&nbsp;We do extend protection to purchasers in the event there is a claimed patent infringement that pertains to&nbsp;our portion of the final product that is
purchased.&nbsp;We also carry product and general liability insurance for protection in such cases. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Competition </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our commercial and military business is highly competitive. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In the commercial market,&nbsp;we have significant competition.&nbsp;As a result,&nbsp;we may not be able to maintain current profit
margins due to price erosion.&nbsp;We believe, however, that our product knowledge, patented technology, ability to respond quickly to customer requirements, positive customer relations, technical background, industry experience and implementation
of our global manufacturing strategy and cost reduction efforts are major&nbsp;operational&nbsp;areas where&nbsp;we are able to effectively compete.</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In the military market,&nbsp;we must compete with other companies, some being larger and some smaller than&nbsp;we are&nbsp;and acting as
suppliers of similar products to prime government contractors.&nbsp;We believe that our knowledge of the procurement process, engineering and technical support, price and delivery are major competitive factors in the military market.&nbsp;We believe
that we have competitive strengths in all of these areas due to senior management&#146;s involvement in the government procurement process and experience in the design engineering requirements for military equipment.&nbsp;A&nbsp;substantial portion
of spare parts procurement by the U.S. Department of Defense is set aside for small business concerns, which are defined in general as entities that do not exceed 750 employees. Because&nbsp;we&nbsp;are classified as a small business
concern,&nbsp;we qualify for such set aside procurements for which larger competitors are not qualified.&nbsp;The entry barriers to the military market are significant because of the need, in most cases, for products to pass stringent government
tests and qualifications. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">8 </FONT></P>

significantly from the first half&nbsp;of&nbsp;the fiscal year compared to the second half of the fiscal year.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">None

United States and Honduras,&nbsp;our reliance upon a foreign manufacturing facility subjects&nbsp;us to risks inherent in international operations.&nbsp;Competition for management, technical, manufacturing, sales and support personnel is intense,
and there can be no assurance that&nbsp;we will be successful in attracting or retaining such personnel. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Executive Officers of the

<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Raymond B. Wood</B>, a founder of the Company, has been a Director of the Company and Senior Vice President and Director of Government

its acquisition by Square D Company. During this time, he held the position of General Manager of Electromagnetic Industries of Georgia Inc., the systems manufacturing plant for military products such as diesel generating systems, generators,
controls, semi-trailers, etc. Previous assignments included service as Project and Design Engineer for military products produced by Electromagnetic Industries Inc. Mr. Wood is a charter member of the industries association, Electrical Generating
Systems Association (&#147;<B>EGSA</B>&#148;), has served on its Board of Directors and has been the Chairman of the Government Liaison Committee for over&nbsp;30 years.&nbsp;Mr.&nbsp;Wood is also a member of the U.S. Naval Institute and the
National Defense Industrial Association. For over&nbsp;45 years, he has been involved in design, manufacture and qualification conformance evaluation for listing by the Department of Defense, marketing and product application concerning control and
measurement of electric power for Mobile Ground Power Military Engine Generator Systems, and electrical power controls for Naval Shipboard and Military Armored Tracked Vehicle application.&nbsp;During such period, Mr.&nbsp;Wood has had
extensive&nbsp;experience with the military procurement, contract administration, engineering and test qualifying locations, as well as with the government prime contractors to the Department of Defense. Mr. Wood has served on numerous ad hoc
committees for military engine generator specification review requirements and is frequently consulted for solutions to problems encountered with military engine generator systems by both the military and prime contractors to the Department of
Defense. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Thomas G. Archbold </B>was appointed, effective as of November&nbsp;17, 2008, our interim Chief Financial Officer

of Engineering and has been an executive officer of TRC since August, 2008. With over 22 years of experience in electronics hardware and firmware, he most recently was Director of Engineering at both Laird Technologies (electromagnetic shielding)
and S. L. Waber/S. L. Industries (surge protection). Mr.&nbsp;Tilghman also spent 3 years as a full time Professor of Electrical Engineering at Lehigh Carbon Community College and as an Adjunct BSET Coordinator/Professor for Temple University. Prior
to this, he spent five years with General Electric and served as a Project Leader and AFCI Program Manager at GE Circuit Breaker division. Mr.&nbsp;Tilghman is a graduate of GE Crotonville&#146;s school for New Managers. He also spent six years with
Deltech/Ingersol Rand as a Design Engineer for embedded control systems for large compressed air equipment. Mr.&nbsp;Tilghman holds an AASEE, BSEE, MS-Engineering Management/EE and is pursuing a Ph.D. in Engineering Management. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="font-size:18px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="9%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><A NAME="toc23282_3"></A>ITEM&nbsp;1A.</B></FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>RISK FACTORS </B></FONT></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Stockholders and investors&nbsp;should carefully consider the risk factors described below, together with the other information
contained&nbsp;in this&nbsp;Annual Report, before making any investment&nbsp;decision with respect to&nbsp;our&nbsp;securities.&nbsp;The risks and uncertainties described below are not the only ones we face.&nbsp;If any of the following risks occur,
our business, financial condition, or results of operation could be significantly impacted and the trading price of our common stock could decline:</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Our growth is subject to a number of economic risks. </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">As widely reported, financial markets in the United States, Europe and Asia have been experiencing extreme disruption in recent months,
including, among other things, extreme volatility in security prices, severely diminished liquidity and credit availability, rating downgrades of certain investments and declining valuations of others. Governments have taken unprecedented actions
intended to address extreme market conditions that include severely restricted credit and declines in real estate values. While currently these conditions have not impaired our ability to access credit markets and finance our operations, there can
be no assurance that there will not be a further deterioration in financial markets and confidence in major economies. These economic developments affect businesses such as ours in a number of ways. The current tightening of credit in financial
markets adversely affects the ability of customers and suppliers to obtain financing for significant purchases and operations and could result in a decrease in or cancellation of orders for our products and services. Our business is also adversely
affected by decreases in the general level of economic activity, such as decreases in business and consumer spending, purchase of electrical safety equipment by consumers and businesses and military procurement. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Failure to achieve our&nbsp;operating strategy could negatively affect our revenue and profits.</I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our operating strategy is to grow revenue and improve gross margin in&nbsp;our military, recreational vehicle, industrial, and consumer
markets as well as closely aligned markets if they share similar products or have other synergies.&nbsp;We&nbsp;plan to achieve these growth goals through internal development of new products, acquisitions, strategic partnerships and
licensing.&nbsp;We have undertaken a number of initiatives to lower costs, improve asset turnover and reduce our risk.&nbsp;These initiatives include, but are not limited to, product line simplification, establishing product platforms in design,
greater utilization of our operations in Honduras, utilizing new designs and operations software to improve quality, accelerate product development and reduce the cost of redesigned products. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Any or all of these objectives may not be realized or, if realized, may not result in increased revenue, profitability or market
presence. Executing&nbsp;our strategy may also place a strain on&nbsp;our production, information technology systems and other resources.</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We rely on the availability of substantial working capital to finance our growth and meet our capital
requirements.</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our ability to continue the growth of our business requires a large amount of working capital.&nbsp;If
we are unable to fund this growth, we may not be able to compete effectively. Our requirement for capital depends on the market&#146;s acceptance of our products, the growth of our marketing effort, our ability to expand our customer base, our need
for additional capital equipment to adopt new manufacturing methods and new products and for future acquisitions.&nbsp;We cannot be sure that additional financing, if needed, will be available or if such financing will be on favorable
terms.&nbsp;Without access to these additional funds, we may not be able to remain competitive. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">10 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>The unavailability of and cost increases in raw materials and components could
negatively affect our revenue and profits.</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Raw materials and components constitute a significant portion of&nbsp;our
cost of sales. Factors that are largely beyond&nbsp;our control, such as movements in commodity prices for the specific materials required, may affect the future cost of raw materials and components. As an example,&nbsp;our products require a
substantial amount of plastic. Because the primary resource used in manufactured plastics is petroleum, the cost and availability of plastic varies to a great extent with the price of petroleum.&nbsp;We have experienced fluctuations in prices of
plastic, as well as steel, aluminum and especially copper, which could continue in fiscal&nbsp;2011. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In addition,&nbsp;the
inability of&nbsp;our suppliers to timely deliver raw materials or components could be disruptive and costly.&nbsp;If we are unable&nbsp;to obtain raw materials on a timely basis at an affordable cost or if we experience any significant delays or
interruptions of supply, our financial results could be significantly impacted. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We depend upon a limited number of key
suppliers.</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We purchase a significant volume of products from contract manufacturers in China.&nbsp;In fiscal 2010, for
example,&nbsp;our purchases from vendors in China were $0.9 million and we expect this amount to decrease in fiscal 2011 as we have exited the RAC business.&nbsp;The purchase price for these products is set in U.S. dollars.&nbsp;If the exchange rate
between the U.S. dollar and Chinese yuan changes so that the yuan appreciates significantly against the dollar, the cost of building our products could increase significantly.&nbsp;We anticipate that outsource providers will play key roles in our
manufacturing operations.&nbsp;Although we aim at selecting reputable providers, it is possible that one or more of these providers could fail to perform as we expect and such failure could have an adverse impact on our business.&nbsp;Because of the
role of our outsource providers, we will need to monitor the performance of these suppliers and adopt new procedures to deal with and manage the performance of these outsource providers.&nbsp;Any delay or failure in the implementation of our
operational changes and monitoring of these relationships could adversely affect our customer relationships and/or have a negative effect on our operating results. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>The loss of or significant decrease in sales to large customers would adversely affect our revenue and profits.</I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We rely upon a continuous flow of new orders from&nbsp;our large customers.&nbsp;Failure to obtain anticipated orders or delays or
cancellations of orders or significant pressure to reduce prices from key customers could have a material adverse effect on us.&nbsp;Our largest customer accounted for 33% of our net revenue for the year ended March&nbsp;31, 2010. Our largest
customer also contracted with us through various subcontractors. If those revenues were included, our largest customer accounted for 37% of our revenue in fiscal 2010. Any reduction in, or cancellation of, orders placed by this customer would at
least temporarily, and possibly longer, cause a material reduction in our net revenue, income from operations and net income. In addition, as a result of the desire to more closely manage inventory levels, there is a growing trend in business,
especially in our commercial markets, to make purchases on a &#147;just-in-time&#148; basis. This requires&nbsp;us to shorten&nbsp;our lead time for production in certain cases and more closely anticipate demand, which could in the future require
the carrying of additional inventories or require additional expenses to expedite delivery. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Our long-term contracts may
not be renewed.</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We currently have contracts with the U.S. military to provide control equipment used in engine
generator systems. These contracts currently run through 2012.&nbsp;We also are a key supplier of control equipment to DRS, a prime contractor with the U.S. military.&nbsp;As a result of the war in Iraq, demand for these military products has
remained strong.&nbsp;If the war ends or significantly winds down or if our contracts with the military expire and are not renewed, demand for these products could be greatly reduced. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We may face cancellations, reductions or delays in our product orders that could adversely affect our operating results.</I></B>
</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">From time to time we receive commercial&nbsp;orders from customers that require us to manufacture products on short notice
and have such products available for shipment, even though the order may be reduced, cancelled or delayed.&nbsp;Some orders may be designed to meet the unique needs of a particular customer.&nbsp;As a result, changes, delays or cancellations of
orders could result in an inventory of unsalable products and possible inventory write-downs that could adversely affect our operating results. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Adverse changes in the operations of global manufacturing facilities could decrease our manufacturing capacity.</I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We manufacture a significant number of products in Honduras and obtain a significant proportion of the raw materials and sub-assembly
components used in the manufacturing of&nbsp;our products outside the United States.&nbsp;International operations are subject to risks including, among others: </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">potentially adverse tax consequences; and </FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
